The daily field guide for digital media

AI Media Scout

2026-11-02 · All platforms · Open-source software

Today completes the new weekly source-review cycle for all 1,512 published tools across every tier, platform and host: 1,512 reviewed, zero untouched entries remaining. This is a dated, bounded source review, not a hands-on or exhaustive dependency audit. Seventeen earlier Experimental labels moved to Quality unverified: five current reasons no longer justify the overall label and twelve could not be confirmed from available current sources. Missing current evidence is not proof of poor quality. The current library retains three Recommended and four Ready to try tools within their specific reviewed workflows; other gaps remain explicit. The weekly batch reopened 122 checks and closed two. GitHub API quota failures, unavailable repositories, Codeberg challenge pages, GitLab 429 responses and failed supplementary URLs are archived; affected checks remain unknown rather than being counted as passed. Rushcut now has an AGPL-3.0-or-later code notice with a narrow VEGAS permission, while its earlier MIT releases remain historical. Twenty-five earlier screened tools now receive complete installation/use/requirements guides: no new tool is added solely on metadata. The guides span kinetic art, diagrams, speech, film, mobile/robot avatars, fabrication, Gaussian splats and game assets. They distinguish software grants, model rights, proprietary hosts and paid services. All platforms receive equal research treatment, with compatibility stated only where documented. No discovered app was installed or executed. New repository candidates remain private when a complete current README/license chain could not be obtained; four selected public-page attempts also failed. The complete retained catalog, including pilot discoveries, remains available for later reviews; there is no fixed daily profile quota. Verified collection: 672/672 repository queries attempted; 672 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 121 bounded/truncated. All 18 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Separate from successful search queries, 537 watchlist fetches and 1231 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Profiles are based on current primary documentation. Creative quality and performance claims are attributed to the developers unless hands-on testing is explicitly recorded. Unknown hardware requirements stay unknown. Code, model weights, hosted services, and required proprietary hosts can have different terms.

How it uses AI

A kinetic-flower installation connects DeepFace and a ViT facial-expression classifier to a Python/Flask host and distributed ESP32 motor, servo, eye-display and light nodes. OpenCV tracking and a damped state machine connect camera observations to OSC or serial movement. [1]

Introduction

A kinetic-flower installation connects DeepFace and a ViT facial-expression classifier to a Python/Flask host and distributed ESP32 motor, servo, eye-display and light nodes. OpenCV tracking and a damped state machine connect camera observations to OSC or serial movement. [1]

What it is good for

Studying embodied audience interaction and adapting a camera-driven kinetic garden, with an inspectable host/dashboard/node architecture. [1]

Demo & examples

python -m pip install -r python_host/requirements.txt
python -m python_host.main --port 15000

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Open the host dashboard, choose a camera and confirm tracking before connecting moving hardware.
  2. Identify the correct flower-node firmware and configure discovery/gateway or USB serial.
  3. Inspect expression confidence, thresholds, decay and cooldown; map stable states to bounded motion.
  4. Record/replay a small motion sequence and inspect tracking and actuator behavior before audience use.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Camera-capable host plus the selected ESP32 boards, Wi-Fi/USB connection, appropriate power/motor drivers, motors/servos and eye/LED hardware. RAM, VRAM, storage and universal actuator specifications are not documented.
Software
Python, Flask, OpenCV, project requirements, DeepFace and the separately downloaded yst007/vit-emotion model; ESP32 firmware toolchains and display/motor libraries depend on the chosen node. Exact Python version and complete cross-platform matrix are not documented.
Platforms
Host platform matrix not documented; the author reports a backup MacBook in the exhibition.; ESP32 nodes are project-specific hardware; this is not a browser-only installation.

How it uses AI

A CLI and agent skill let an AI coding agent plan and inspect a 3D-print job represented in an editable deli.toml. The open front end embeds PrusaSlicer’s slicing library and converts Orca profiles; the agent supplies the AI reasoning. [1]

What it is good for

Preparing reviewable slicing jobs and teaching an agent to inspect material/printer choices before generating a print artifact. [1]

Platforms
Linux x64/ARM64 with glibc2.28+ wheels.; macOS11+ Apple Silicon or10.15+ Intel wheels; installer support differs.; Windows through WSL; native Windows execution is not established.

First project

  3. Render a small draft through the documented make_edit route.
  4. Review every technical check and inspect the final picture/captions/audio manually.

ComfyUI-Idol — Brie-Wensleydale

Documented creative workflow; quality confidence assessed separately · Apache-2.0 · recently-created

The repository was created on 2026-10-30. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

Docs checked 2026-11-02 · 1 GitHub stars · Documentation review; installation not tested

Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Making fast object-splat studies from one generated image or photograph and inspecting the actual crop and imagined hidden sides.

Evidence checked 2026-11-02; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Open-source software license · verified: The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [2]
  • Creative results · documented: Eight owner-created object examples and RTX4090 measurements are documented. The upstream optimized19.1-second number differs from this wrapper’s27–31-second generation; neither is a universal performance guarantee. [1]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: Clone into ComfyUI/custom_nodes, install the two documented packages with ComfyUI’s interpreter and --no-deps, restart and open the simple/turntable example graph. The first Idol Loader run downloads about6.8GB. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
  • Independent use · unknown: Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
  • Models, services and costs · documented: Apache-2.0 covers this node pack. Idol, DINOv3, FLUX.2 VAE and BiRefNet models have separate grants; ungated download does not remove those terms. Local inference and storage; no mandatory paid generation API is documented. [1]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Unseen surfaces are invented; wide orbits can reveal holes/smears, thin structures disappear, and diffuse-only color does not represent glass/metals faithfully. A splat lacks mesh normals, UVs and rigging. Lowering Gaussian count does not reduce the reported peak VRAM.

How it uses AI

A ComfyUI node pack wraps the Idol image-to-3D Gaussian model and returns a native SPLAT, a preprocessed reference and an optional PLY path. Core ComfyUI nodes handle turntables and export. [1]

Introduction

A ComfyUI node pack wraps the Idol image-to-3D Gaussian model and returns a native SPLAT, a preprocessed reference and an optional PLY path. Core ComfyUI nodes handle turntables and export. [1]

What it is good for

Making fast object-splat studies from one generated image or photograph and inspecting the actual crop and imagined hidden sides. [1]

Demo & examples

Eight owner-created object examples and RTX4090 measurements are documented. The upstream optimized19.1-second number differs from this wrapper’s27–31-second generation; neither is a universal performance guarantee. [1]

Install

Clone into ComfyUI/custom_nodes, install the two documented packages with ComfyUI’s interpreter and --no-deps, restart and open the simple/turntable example graph. The first Idol Loader run downloads about6.8GB. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Clone into ComfyUI/custom_nodes, install the two documented packages with ComfyUI’s interpreter and --no-deps, restart and open the simple/turntable example graph. The first Idol Loader run downloads about6.8GB.
git clone https://github.com/Brie-Wensleydale/ComfyUI-Idol
python_embeded\python.exe -m pip install --no-deps tensordict plyfile

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Open idol_simple.json or idol_turntable.json in ComfyUI.
  2. Select a clean single-object image and the correct real/synthetic crop mode.
  3. Inspect the preprocessed reference and a modest orbit before trusting hidden geometry.
  4. Save the splat or use separate core mesh conversion; unload cached weights when finished.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Author measured7.2GB reserved peak and3.7GB resident VRAM on a24GB RTX4090; the node requests9GB from ComfyUI. About6.8GB model downloads. Host-RAM and universal device minima are not documented.
Software
Python3.10+ and a compatible ComfyUI with native SPLAT support; tensordict and plyfile. Use the actual ComfyUI interpreter; the shown embedded-Python command is a Windows-portable example.
Platforms
RTX4090/ComfyUI measurements are reported.; A complete Windows/macOS/Linux and GPU-family acceptance matrix is not supplied.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Apache-2.0 covers this node pack. Idol, DINOv3, FLUX.2 VAE and BiRefNet models have separate grants; ungated download does not remove those terms.
  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 covers this node pack. Idol, DINOv3, FLUX.2 VAE and BiRefNet models have separate grants; ungated download does not remove those terms.
  • Cost: Local inference and storage; no mandatory paid generation API is documented.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

Unseen surfaces are invented; wide orbits can reveal holes/smears, thin structures disappear, and diffuse-only color does not represent glass/metals faithfully. A splat lacks mesh normals, UVs and rigging. Lowering Gaussian count does not reduce the reported peak VRAM. [1]

  4. Inspect the source-view render and turntable, then export PLY and manually check unseen sides.

How it uses AI

A Python inference server drives Unity Humanoid characters using NVIDIA MotionBricks; an editor baker converts generated motion into ordinary .anim clips for offline game playback. [1]

Introduction

A Python inference server drives Unity Humanoid characters using NVIDIA MotionBricks; an editor baker converts generated motion into ordinary .anim clips for offline game playback. [1]

What it is good for

Bake short pose or path-driven character clips on an NVIDIA host, then ship playback without mobile-device inference. [1]

Demo & examples

The owner records a November 1 RTX 3060 first-boot pass and later Clip Baker pass with Timeline DefaultMale. These owner checks are not broad retargeting or animation-quality validation. [1]

Install

Set up the official MotionBricks uv environment on an NVIDIA GPU host with Python 3.12, CUDA 12 and TensorRT. Run the bridge server inside that environment with injected FastAPI/uvicorn. Copy runtime C# files into a Unity 2021.3+ project and editor files under an Editor folder. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Set up the official MotionBricks uv environment on an NVIDIA GPU host with Python 3.12, CUDA 12 and TensorRT. Run the bridge server inside that environment with injected FastAPI/uvicorn. Copy runtime C# files into a Unity 2021.3+ project and editor files under an Editor folder.
uv run --with fastapi --with uvicorn python /path/to/motionbricks-unity-bridge/server/motionbricks_server.py --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Verify /info and /health on the inference server.
  2. Use a Humanoid Avatar with an empty Animator controller and validate the T-pose mirror mapping.
  3. Configure a short Clip Baker take, bake the .anim and inspect foot contact, root motion and proportions before shipping.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
An NVIDIA GPU is required for inference; an owner RTX 3060 pass is documented but VRAM/RAM/storage minima are not. The mobile game consumes baked clips without running this model.
Software
Python 3.12, NVIDIA CUDA 12/TensorRT and the official MotionBricks uv environment. Unity 2021.3+ with a Humanoid Avatar; first startup builds a cached TensorRT plan.
Platforms
NVIDIA CUDA host; host OS not specified by this bridge; Unity editor/client; Mobile playback through offline baked clips, not on-device inference

  • Weights: Apache-2.0 covers the bridge. Official MotionBricks code is separately Apache-2.0; pretrained ONNX weights use NVIDIA Open Model License with attribution/trustworthy-AI conditions. Unity and imported character assets have separate terms.
  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 covers the bridge. Official MotionBricks code is separately Apache-2.0; pretrained ONNX weights use NVIDIA Open Model License with attribution/trustworthy-AI conditions. Unity and imported character assets have separate terms.
  • Cost: Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

How it uses AI

An Android overlay combines a VRM avatar, Vosk speech recognition and local SmolLM2 conversation on a temi robot, with speech voiced by temi itself. [1]

Introduction

An Android overlay combines a VRM avatar, Vosk speech recognition and local SmolLM2 conversation on a temi robot, with speech voiced by temi itself. [1]

What it is good for

Create an English-speaking embodied character overlay and prototype an exhibition robot companion without cloud conversation inference. [1]

Demo & examples

The README documents the floating pet and status interface, rendering optimizations and local voice loop. Some transparency behavior remains untested on the physical robot; no independent avatar/voice acceptance was established. [1]

Install

Open the Android project in Android Studio, allow its pinned llama.cpp/NDK and CMake 3.22.1 setup, build and ADB-install the APK. Open the robot ADB port and use the asset installer to place the local model, Vosk voice model and licensed VRM; grant microphone and overlay permissions. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Open the Android project in Android Studio, allow its pinned llama.cpp/NDK and CMake 3.22.1 setup, build and ADB-install the APK. Open the robot ADB port and use the asset installer to place the local model, Vosk voice model and licensed VRM; grant microphone and overlay permissions.
adb install -r app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk
scripts/install-assets.sh <temi-ip> path/to/avatar.vrm

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Launch once from the robot launcher and inspect Status for model, voice and avatar state.
  2. Say hey mate in a quiet room and test a short dialogue.
  3. Check the avatar blink and A/aa expressions; use a smaller VRM if rendering is slow.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Requires a temi robot. The author recommends a VRM about 25 MB or less with textures at most 1024 px; the Vosk model is about 40 MB. Minimum system RAM and complete disk footprint are not documented.
Software
Android Studio, NDK/CMake 3.22.1 and ADB. Targets temi V2 Android 6.0.1/API 23 and V3 Android 11; English Vosk, llama.cpp and Filament.
Platforms
temi V2 Android 6.0.1/API 23; temi V3 Android 11, with microphone-sharing caveat

How it uses AI

A Windows mosaic-template editor optionally enlarges artwork with a bundled CUNet AI model, then tiles the result into full-color life-size Letter pages or PDF. [1]

Introduction

A Windows mosaic-template editor optionally enlarges artwork with a bundled CUNet AI model, then tiles the result into full-color life-size Letter pages or PDF. [1]

What it is good for

Compare original and AI-enlarged artwork, specify physical dimensions and prepare a calibrated paper template for handmade mosaics. [1]

Demo & examples

The README shows a rendered bilingual WPF editor and describes real CPU/GPU inference and PDF checks. Physical printer measurements and independent artist acceptance remain open; an accepted print job is not a measured physical print. [1]

Install

There is no downloadable installer yet. Build on Windows x64 with .NET SDK 10.0.402; dotnet run starts the Release app. Inno Setup 7.1.0 plus Build-Release.ps1 creates a self-contained unsigned installer. The first restore needs Internet; the packaged core workflow includes its AI workers/model and runs offline. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. There is no downloadable installer yet. Build on Windows x64 with .NET SDK 10.0.402; dotnet run starts the Release app. Inno Setup 7.1.0 plus Build-Release.ps1 creates a self-contained unsigned installer. The first restore needs Internet; the packaged core workflow includes its AI workers/model and runs offline.
dotnet run --project src/Mosaic.App/Mosaic.App.csproj -c Release -r win-x64 -p:RestoreLockedMode=true
.\scripts\Build-Release.ps1

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Open a JPEG, PNG, WebP or BMP and enter the intended finished dimensions.
  2. Compare Standard enlargement with Enhanced CUNet output and preserve the original.
  3. Run the four-sheet printer calibration, measure it, then print/export at Actual size or 100 percent and save the .mosaic project.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Windows 10 build 19041+ or Windows 11 x64. .NET SDK 10.0.402 for building; Inno Setup 7.1.0 for the installer. Vulkan driver enables GPU enhancement and a bundled CPU worker provides fallback.
Platforms
Windows 10 build 19041+ x64; Windows 11 x64
